## 2. Quantum Thermodynamics Of The Caldeira-Leggett Model With Non-equilibrium Gaussian Reservoirs

- **Score**: 9.2 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Quantum  |  **Feed**: Quantum
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-06-15
- **Authors**: Vasco Cavina And Massimiliano Esposito
- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-06-15-2136/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-06-15-2136/)
- **Tags**: `application`  `noise`  `thermodynamics`  `dissipation`  `dynamics`

### TL;DR

A non-equilibrium Caldeira-Leggett model with squeezed and displaced reservoirs is introduced, enabling work extraction and a fluctuation theorem for heat statistics.

### Motivation

To extend the Caldeira-Leggett model to non-equilibrium reservoirs, allowing for work extraction and studying thermodynamic symmetries beyond the standard equilibrium assumption.

### Method

The reservoirs are modeled as collections of squeezed and displaced thermal modes; a modified Keldysh contour technique is used to compute full heat statistics.

### Result

Strongly displaced/squeezed reservoirs generate effective time dependence and pure work; a fluctuation theorem for energy balance is proven, and quantum-classical correspondence is shown.

### Conclusion

The model provides a versatile framework for quantum thermodynamics with non-equilibrium reservoirs, reconciling stochastic time dependence with the second law.

*Keywords: Caldeira-Leggett model, non-equilibrium reservoirs, fluctuation theorem, quantum thermodynamics, squeezed states*

- **Link**: [https://www.nature.com/articles/s41534-026-01318-8](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41534-026-01318-8)
- **Tags**: `Gibbs state preparation`  `variational quantum algorithm`  `trapped-ion quantum computing`  `quantum thermodynamics`  `free energy minimization`

### TL;DR

This paper presents a variational quantum algorithm for preparing Gibbs states on trapped-ion devices, combining quantum and classical resources to approximate thermal states.

### Motivation

Gibbs states are essential for quantum thermodynamics and many-body physics, but preparing them on near-term quantum devices is challenging due to noise and limited coherence.

### Method

The authors propose a variational hybrid quantum-classical algorithm that minimizes the free energy using a parameterized quantum circuit, with gradients estimated via the parameter-shift rule and a classical optimizer.

### Result

Numerical simulations and experiments on a trapped-ion processor demonstrate successful preparation of Gibbs states for small systems, with fidelity comparable to state-of-the-art methods.

### Conclusion

The variational approach offers a scalable path to Gibbs state preparation on current quantum hardware, enabling studies of thermal properties and quantum thermodynamics.

*Keywords: Gibbs state preparation, variational quantum algorithm, trapped-ion quantum computing, quantum thermodynamics, free energy minimization*

- **Tags**: `thermal state preparation`  `adiabatic quantum computing`  `entropy-noise relation`  `quantum thermodynamics`  `noisy intermediate-scale quantum (NISQ)`

### TL;DR

This paper proposes an adiabatic protocol to prepare thermal states on noisy quantum computers and derives an entropy-noise relation.

### Motivation

Preparing thermal states is crucial for quantum thermodynamics and many-body physics, but current methods are limited by noise and hardware constraints.

### Method

The authors design a quasi-adiabatic evolution that gradually couples the system to a simulated bath, and analyze the trade-off between entropy production and noise using a Lindblad master equation.

### Result

They derive a fundamental bound relating the entropy increase to the noise strength and evolution time, and demonstrate the protocol's feasibility on current noisy devices.

### Conclusion

The work provides a practical framework for thermal state preparation and a new thermodynamic relation that connects entropy, noise, and adiabaticity.

*Keywords: thermal state preparation, adiabatic quantum computing, entropy-noise relation, quantum thermodynamics, noisy intermediate-scale quantum (NISQ)*

- **Tags**: `noise`  `quantum sensing`  `Heisenberg limit`  `indefinite causal order`  `quantum error correction`

### TL;DR

This paper proposes a quantum sensing protocol that uses indefinite causal order (ICO) to achieve Heisenberg-limited scaling even under noise, circumventing no-go theorems.

### Motivation

Heisenberg-limited quantum sensing is fundamentally limited by noise; standard quantum error correction is too resource-intensive for sensing tasks.

### Method

The authors introduce an error correction scheme based on indefinite causal order (ICO), where operations are applied in a superposition of orders, to protect the sensing probe from noise without requiring ancilla qubits or frequent measurements.

### Result

The ICO-based protocol achieves Heisenberg-limited scaling in the presence of depolarizing noise, with a factor of 2 improvement in precision over the standard sequential strategy.

### Conclusion

ICO provides a practical and resource-efficient way to realize Heisenberg-limited sensing in realistic noisy devices, opening new avenues for quantum metrology.

*Keywords: quantum sensing, Heisenberg limit, indefinite causal order, quantum error correction, quantum metrology*

### TL;DR

This paper shows that sequential local state discrimination can sustain entanglement indefinitely, even when the initial state is arbitrarily weakly entangled.

### Motivation

Entanglement is a key resource for quantum technologies, but local state discrimination typically degrades it. The authors aim to reconcile these two fundamental aspects by exploring sequential discrimination protocols.

### Method

The authors analyze a sequential local quantum state discrimination protocol where multiple parties perform local measurements on a shared bipartite state, and they derive conditions under which entanglement is preserved or even enhanced.

### Result

They prove that for certain sets of orthogonal bipartite states, sequential local discrimination can be performed without consuming entanglement, and in fact the entanglement can be sustained or increased arbitrarily.

### Conclusion

### Method

The authors analyze two quantum wave packets, one falling into a black hole, and use entanglement measures to distinguish separable from entangled states, considering the effects of the event horizon.

### Result

They find that entanglement can be detected even when one wave packet is beyond the event horizon, provided the other is observed outside.

### Conclusion

## 16. Bridging The Daemonic Gap En Route To Charge Multimode Batteries Via A Single Auxiliary

- **Score**: 8.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Phys. Rev. A  |  **Feed**: Physical Review A
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-07-07
- **Authors**: Chandrima B. Pushpan And Amit Kumar Pal
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/jr71-mkfl](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/jr71-mkfl)
- **Tags**: `multimode`  `quantum battery`  `daemonic ergotropy`  `work extraction`  `quantum thermodynamics`

### TL;DR

This paper demonstrates that daemonic advantage can be harnessed to charge a quantum battery using a single auxiliary charger, bridging the gap between daemonic ergotropy and maximum extractable work.

### Motivation

Quantum batteries are limited by the maximum extractable work (ergotropy). Daemonic advantage, typically requiring multiple auxiliary systems, can enhance work extraction. This work aims to achieve daemonic advantage with a single auxiliary, making it more practical.

### Method

The authors consider a quantum battery collectively time-evolved with an auxiliary charger, then extract energy by tracing out the charger. They define the difference between minimum daemonic ergotropy and maximum ergotropy as the 'daemonic gap' and show it can be bridged via optimal measurements on the auxiliary.

### Result

They show that by performing an optimal measurement on the auxiliary, the daemonic gap can be closed, allowing the battery to reach its maximum ergotropy. This is demonstrated for various battery-charger models, including multimode batteries.

### Conclusion

Daemonic advantage can be achieved with a single auxiliary, enabling efficient charging of quantum batteries. This bridges the gap between daemonic and standard ergotropy, offering a practical route to enhance quantum battery performance.

*Keywords: quantum battery, daemonic ergotropy, work extraction, quantum thermodynamics, auxiliary charger*

The purity is expressed in terms of transition probabilities from an echo-type forward-backward evolution sequence, avoiding random-noise averaging; the protocol is extended to out-of-time-order correlators.

The protocols are shown to be practically implementable on superconducting qubit platforms and in cavity-QED trapped ultracold gases.

### Conclusion

The echo protocol provides a practical, efficient way to measure Rényi entropy and OTOCs, advancing experimental quantum information processing.

This paper tightly characterizes the query complexity of quantum phase estimation with advice states, providing nearly matching upper and lower bounds for all parameter regimes.

### Motivation

Phase estimation is a fundamental subroutine in quantum computing, but its cost when given imperfect advice (states with partial overlap with the top eigenspace) was not well understood. The authors aim to close this gap.

The authors develop new algorithms and prove lower bounds using the polynomial method with trigonometric polynomials, as well as reductions from the unitary recurrence time problem.

### TL;DR

This paper develops a complexity-theoretic framework for non-local quantum computation, showing equivalence between two key tasks and providing efficient protocols for functions in certain complexity classes.

### Motivation

Characterizing the entanglement cost of non-local quantum computation (NLQC) is known to be difficult and related to major complexity theory problems. The authors aim to circumvent these obstructions by studying relative hardness of NLQC tasks via resource-efficient reductions.

### Method

The authors introduce reductions between NLQC tasks, analogous to complexity theory reductions, and prove that the two best-studied tasks, f-measure and f-route, are equivalent under polynomial overhead reductions. They also analyze other NLQC tasks and their relationships.

They prove that f-measure and f-route are equivalent under polynomial overhead reductions, obtain sub-exponential upper bounds on f-measure for all functions, and provide efficient protocols for functions in the complexity class P.

### TL;DR

This paper improves the convergence rate of the quantum Zeno effect to arbitrarily high order using a multi-product formula, with applications to projected Hamiltonian and Lindbladian dynamics.

### Motivation

The standard quantum Zeno effect has a limited convergence rate (order 1), which restricts its efficiency in approximating projected dynamics. Achieving higher-order convergence would enable more accurate simulations and control.

### Method

The authors combine a modified Chernoff lemma, an adapted Dunford-Segal approximation, holomorphic functional calculus, and Chebyshev interpolation to construct a multi-product formula that yields arbitrarily high-order convergence.

### Result

The multi-product Zeno effect provides a systematic way to enhance the accuracy of Zeno-based approximations, with potential applications in quantum control and simulation.

### Motivation

Understanding entanglement Hamiltonians is crucial for characterizing quantum many-body systems, but their experimental extraction is challenging. Variational methods offer a practical approach, yet their convergence properties and reliability need systematic study.

### Method

The authors interpret the cost functional as an integral, enabling iterative quadrature schemes to reduce measurements. They also introduce a modified ansatz to capture deviations from the Bisognano-Wichmann form in lattice models.

### Result

The iterative quadrature reduces required measurements by over an order of magnitude even with noise. The modified ansatz improves convergence and trainability, and provides a cost-function-level diagnostic for quantum phase transitions. ### Conclusion

Variational entanglement Hamiltonians can be efficiently optimized and provide reliable spectral information, making them a useful tool for studying topological phases and quantum criticality.

### TL;DR

Lorentz boosts can split a single relaxation mode into a continuum of excitations, challenging the naive expectation of time dilation in relativistic relaxation processes.

### Motivation

Understanding how relaxation processes behave under Lorentz boosts is crucial for relativistic thermodynamics and non-equilibrium statistical mechanics.

### Method

The author analyzes the effect of Lorentz boosts on relaxation spectra using relativistic kinetic theory and the concept of relativity of simultaneity.

### Result

A single relaxation mode is shown to broaden into a continuum of excitations with a width set by the maximum boost factor, contradicting simple time dilation.

### Conclusion

Relaxation spectra are not invariant under boosts, which has implications for relativistic fluid dynamics and the formulation of thermodynamics in moving frames.

### TL;DR

This paper develops a path integral control framework for open quantum systems described by GKLS master equations, enabling efficient open-loop state preparation.

### Motivation

Controlling open quantum systems is crucial for quantum technologies, but optimal control is computationally challenging due to the high-dimensional nature of the density matrix.

### Method

The authors reformulate the deterministic control objective as a stochastic optimal control problem using a trajectory-based representation of the GKLS master equation, then apply path integral control theory to derive an analytical solution.

### Result

They demonstrate that the optimal control can be expressed as an expectation over stochastic trajectories, which can be efficiently computed via Monte Carlo sampling, avoiding the curse of dimensionality.

### Conclusion

The path integral approach provides a scalable method for open-loop control of open quantum systems, with potential applications in quantum state preparation and quantum thermodynamics.

*Keywords: open quantum systems, GKLS master equation, path integral control, stochastic optimal control, quantum state preparation*

### TL;DR

This paper quantifies the quantum-to-classical transition in Kerr nonlinear dynamics under loss, establishing three distinct timescales and showing that negativity is robust against moderate loss.

### Motivation

Understanding the quantum-to-classical transition is fundamental for quantum information processing, and Kerr dynamics is a key nonlinear process. The role of loss in this transition is not well understood.

### Method

The authors analyze the single-mode Kerr Hamiltonian with loss using phase-space methods and negativity as a nonclassicality measure. They derive analytical expressions for the timescales and numerically simulate the dynamics.

### Result

Three timescales are identified: Ehrenfest time, negativity collapse time, and negativity revival time. Negativity is shown to persist beyond the Ehrenfest time even with moderate loss, indicating robustness.

### Conclusion

The quantum-to-classical transition in Kerr dynamics is characterized by multiple timescales, and negativity can survive loss, which has implications for quantum information protocols using Kerr nonlinearities.

*Keywords: Kerr nonlinearity, quantum-to-classical transition, negativity, nonclassicality, open quantum systems*

### TL;DR

This paper proves impossibility results for addressing logical qubits in high-rate CSS codes using local Clifford circuits and physical permutations.

### Motivation

Understanding how to implement logical gates on subsets of logical qubits is crucial for fault-tolerant quantum computation, especially with asymptotically good codes.

### Method

The authors use automorphism theory of CSS codes and combinatorial arguments to derive no-go theorems for addressability under constraints of locality and fault-tolerance.

### Result

They show that for CSS codes with non-zero rate, one cannot address logical X, Z, H, or S gates to a strict subset of logical qubits using 1-local Clifford circuits, and that permuting physical qubits cannot permute logical qubits if the rate exceeds 1/3 and distance is at least 3.

### Conclusion

The work highlights a fundamental trade-off between addressability and code efficiency, opening new directions for designing fault-tolerant quantum circuits.

*Keywords: CSS codes, addressability, logical gates, fault tolerance, automorphisms*

The authors analyze the precision scaling in terms of total particle number for unitary evolutions belonging to SU(2) or SU(1,1) groups. They consider both optimal states in fixed-number subspaces and practical scenarios using expectation values of time-evolved observables restricted to first two moments of generators.

### Result

For SU(2), eigenstates of J_z allow simultaneous Heisenberg scaling for all three parameters. For SU(1,1), analogous states in the equal-number sector achieve similar scaling. In the pragmatic scenario, twin-Fock states enable Heisenberg scaling for two out of three parameters, and two-mode squeezed states allow simultaneous Heisenberg scaling for a two-parameter SU(1,1) estimation.

### Conclusion

The work extends the understanding of multiparameter estimation to non-compact groups and identifies practical states that can achieve Heisenberg scaling, providing guidance for experimental quantum metrology.

*Keywords: multiparameter estimation, Heisenberg scaling, SU(2), SU(1,1), quantum metrology*

### Motivation

The fluctuation-dissipation theorem does not hold in nonequilibrium systems; understanding its violation in domain growth processes is important for characterizing nonequilibrium dynamics.

### Method

The authors use analytical arguments and numerical simulations of the long-range Ising model to study the two-time correlation and response functions, and test the generalized FDT proposed by Cugliandolo and Kurchan.

### Result

They find that the generalized FDT holds with a time-dependent effective temperature that characterizes the aging dynamics, and the violation is quantified by a fluctuation-dissipation ratio that approaches a constant in the long-time limit.

### Conclusion

The generalized FDT provides a useful framework for understanding nonequilibrium dynamics in systems with long-range interactions, and the effective temperature concept is validated.

### TL;DR

This paper proves upper and lower bounds on query complexity and circuit depth for implementing arbitrary quantum unitaries using Grover search.

### Motivation

Understanding the minimal resources (queries, depth) needed to implement arbitrary unitaries is fundamental to quantum computing and circuit optimization.

### Method

The authors reduce unitary implementation to Grover search, constructing circuits that approximate or exactly implement any unitary with given query and depth bounds, and prove matching lower bounds for a class of implementations.

### Result

Any n-qubit unitary can be implemented approximately in O(4^n) time with query access to a classical oracle, and exactly by a circuit of depth O(4^n) with one- and two-qubit gates and O(4^n) ancillae; these bounds are tight for certain implementations.

### Conclusion

The work provides near-optimal resource bounds for universal unitary synthesis, with implications for quantum circuit design and complexity theory.

*Keywords: Grover search, quantum circuit depth, unitary synthesis, query complexity, quantum complexity theory*

### TL;DR

This paper applies machine learning decoding to bivariate bicycle codes, a type of quantum LDPC code, achieving lower logical error rates than BP-OSD on small codes.

### Motivation

Fault-tolerant quantum computing requires efficient decoders; machine learning decoders have shown promise for surface codes but not yet for QLDPC codes, which are more challenging.

### Method

A recurrent, transformer-based neural network is trained to decode circuit-level noise on bivariate bicycle codes, using syndrome measurement data to predict error corrections.

### Result

On the [[144,12,12]] BB code, the ML decoder achieves logical error rates almost 10 times lower than BP-OSD and is an order of magnitude faster in worst-case runtime; on the [[288,12,18]] code, error rates are worse but speed advantage remains.

### Conclusion

ML decoders can outperform conventional decoders on small QLDPC codes, but scaling to larger codes requires more complex architectures or training procedures.

- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-06-30-2147/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-06-30-2147/)
- **Tags**: `physics`  `experiment`  `prediction`  `Wigner's Friend`  `quantum foundations`

### TL;DR

The paper argues that the Wigner's Friend paradox is not unique to quantum theory and can be reproduced classically, suggesting broader implications for foundations of physics and philosophy.

### Motivation

Recent interest in Wigner's Friend paradox has focused on quantum foundations, but the authors challenge this view by showing the paradox can arise without quantum physics.

### Method

The authors analyze Extended Wigner's Friend scenarios and construct classical thought experiments with agent duplication to demonstrate that the core issue, termed 'Restriction A', is a general feature of physical theories.

### Result

They show that some implications of Wigner's Friend scenarios can be reproduced classically, and that the underlying difficulty applies to other puzzles like the Boltzmann brain problem.

### Conclusion

Wigner's Friend should be studied in a broader context beyond quantum foundations, addressing challenges in obtaining reliable predictions when observations are privately but not intersubjectively verifiable.

*Keywords: Wigner's Friend, quantum foundations, agent duplication, Boltzmann brain, Restriction A*

### TL;DR

This paper presents an open-source Python library for optical layout (PyOpticL) and demonstrates its use for building modular optical systems for trapped-ion quantum computing.

### Motivation

Complex optical design in quantum computing with atoms and trapped ions is hindered by conventional piecewise setup, which prevents modularization and scalability.

### Method

The authors developed PyOpticL, a Python library that uses beam-path simulation and dynamic routing to enable adaptive, path-based layouts. They created modular 'drop-in' optical baseplates for common subsystems and demonstrated a full laser system for strontium trapped ions.

### Result

The system achieved over 99% fidelity single-qubit gates and was used for laser cooling and qubit state detection, demonstrating the viability of the modular approach.

### TL;DR

This paper studies odd-viscous Stokes flow around spheroids in a diatomic gas under a magnetic field, showing shape-based separation via Hall angles.

### Motivation

Understanding how magnetic fields affect transport in neutral diatomic gases (Senftleben-Beenakker effect) and exploiting odd viscosities for particle separation.

### Method

Perturbative expansion around spherical solution and Lorentz reciprocal theorem to compute lift forces on oblate and prolate spheroids for general odd viscosity coefficients.

### Result

Hall angles differ for oblate and prolate spheroids, enabling shape-based separation during sedimentation in a magnetic field.

### Conclusion

The study demonstrates a mechanism to separate particles by shape using odd viscosities in a diatomic gas under a magnetic field.

- **Tags**: `noise`  `transport`  `simulation`  `lead`  `experiment`

### TL;DR

This paper studies how internal parameters of active Brownian particles affect rectified transport in asymmetric ratchets, using a macroscopic experimental model.

### Motivation

Most existing work on active ratchets focuses on structural boundaries; this work shifts to internal properties like mass and center of mass position.

### Method

A vibration-driven vehicle serves as a macroscopic analog of active Brownian particles; mass and center of mass are systematically varied, and long-time dynamics are tracked experimentally and via coarse-grained simulations.

### Result

Increasing mass reduces average velocity and persistence length, leading to a noise-dominated localized state; shifting the center of mass induces a non-monotonic reentrant phase transition in rectification probability.

### Conclusion

Load-induced parameter evolution is a core determinant of macroscopic transport behavior, offering design principles beyond geometric constraints.

*Keywords: active Brownian particles, asymmetric ratchets, rectified transport, vibration-driven vehicle, reentrant phase transition*

### TL;DR

This paper studies chiral graviton modes in fractional Chern insulators, a topic in condensed matter physics with no direct connection to quantum information or thermodynamics.

### Motivation

To determine whether chiral graviton modes, hallmark excitations of fractional quantum Hall liquids, exist in lattice fractional Chern insulators where continuum symmetries are absent.

### Method

Derives a lattice stress tensor operator for the Harper-Hofstadter model and uses matrix product state and exact diagonalization simulations to study graviton modes in a checkerboard lattice model.

### Result

Provides evidence that chiral graviton modes are long-lived excitations in FCIs despite lack of continuous symmetries, with a small intrinsic decay rate compared to energy.

### Conclusion

The results are relevant for exploring graviton modes in solid-state and cold-atom experiments, but the paper does not address quantum information or thermodynamics.

Existing network reconstruction methods focus on binary interactions and dynamics-based data, but real-world data often involve coarse-grained interaction states and multi-body interactions.

### Method

The method formulates an optimization model based on the minimum description length principle, uses MCMC sampling to generate candidate configurations, builds a Bayesian network guided by minimum-entropy, and applies an iterative update strategy.

### Result

Experiments on synthetic and real-world datasets demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed method.

### Conclusion

The proposed Bayesian network reconstruction method can effectively infer higher-order network structures from low-resolution interaction state data.

*Keywords: network reconstruction, higher-order networks, Bayesian network, MCMC, minimum description length*

Interpreting time delays in ultrafast experiments is crucial for understanding electron dynamics, but no consensus exists for low energies or short IR wavelengths.

ASPIRE is a broadly applicable framework for efficient Bayesian inference in gravitational-wave astronomy.

The authors combine self-consistent phonon theory and the Boltzmann transport equation to compute thermal conductivity, analyzing anharmonicity and four-phonon scattering.

To test the foundational cosmological principle of isotropy and homogeneity by comparing the dipole in the matter distribution with the CMB dipole.

Analysis of large catalogs of radio galaxies and quasars to measure the dipole anisotropy and compare it with the CMB dipole.

### Conclusion

- **Tags**: `plasma`  `direct laser acceleration`  `betatron resonance`  `plasma magnetic field`  `hysteresis`

### TL;DR

This paper studies phase-controlled direct laser acceleration using longitudinal variation of a laser-driven quasi-static plasma magnetic field.

### Motivation

To overcome the reversible energy exchange that limits net acceleration in direct laser acceleration (DLA) of electrons.

### Method

A test-electron model with prescribed fields is used to show that a slow longitudinal increase of the quasi-static plasma magnetic field introduces hysteresis in the betatron-to-laser frequency ratio.

### Result

The hysteresis enables phase control of the electron-laser energy exchange and suppresses reversibility, allowing sustained energy gain.

### Conclusion

Longitudinal variation of the plasma magnetic field can qualitatively change DLA, enabling more efficient electron acceleration.

*Keywords: direct laser acceleration, betatron resonance, plasma magnetic field, hysteresis, electron acceleration*

## 490. Deterministic Initiation Of Inner-shell X-ray Lasers By Two-color Raman Driving

- **Score**: 0.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: New J. Phys.  |  **Feed**: New Journal of Physics
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-30
- **Authors**: Carles Serrat
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae7f43](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae7f43)
- **Tags**: `noise`  `dynamics`  `plasma`  `x-ray`  `lasing`

### TL;DR

This paper proposes a deterministic seeding mechanism for inner-shell x-ray lasers using two-color Raman driving, shifting the lasing onset from stochastic noise to a driven coherence.

### Motivation

Conventional inner-shell x-ray lasers are initiated by amplified spontaneous emission (ASE), leading to stochastic temporal onset and phase. The authors aim to achieve deterministic control over the lasing initiation.

### Method

The authors analyze a regime where a resonant two-color Raman drive induces a core-valence coherence at the lasing frequency, acting as a seed. They use a microscopic density-matrix description coupled to a linear Maxwell-Bloch-Langevin propagation model with calibrated spontaneous-emission noise, and derive an analytical criterion separating noise-dominated ASE from coherence-dominated seeded regime.

### Result

An explicit analytical criterion is derived, depending on Raman drive amplitude, small-signal gain, gate duration, and noise strength. Numerical integration confirms the predicted crossover. A quantitative mapping to pump intensities for Ne is provided.

### Conclusion

The mechanism identifies a coherence-dominated operating regime for inner-shell x-ray lasing, shifting the field origin from spontaneous fluctuations to a driven microscopic coherence, while gain dynamics remain unchanged.

*Keywords: inner-shell x-ray laser, Raman driving, coherence seeding, amplified spontaneous emission, Maxwell-Bloch-Langevin model*

---

This paper studies topologically-protected surface plasma waves in non-uniformly magnetized plasma columns, focusing on wave propagation and collisionless damping.

### Motivation

To extend the theory of topologically-protected surface plasma waves from uniformly magnetized to realistic three-dimensional magnetic fields, and to understand wave absorption in non-uniform plasmas.

### Method

Theoretical analysis of wave propagation in a cold plasma column with a non-uniform magnetic field produced by finite-sized coils, including collisionless damping by upper-hybrid resonances.

### Result

TSPWs launched in the direction of decreasing magnetic field are collisionlessly absorbed in a localized evanescent region, with negligible reflection, contrasting with unmagnetized non-uniform plasmas.

### Conclusion

The findings demonstrate robust, reflection-free absorption of TSPWs in realistic magnetic field configurations, which may have applications in plasma heating or wave manipulation.

*Keywords: topologically-protected surface plasma waves, collisionless damping, non-uniform magnetized plasma, upper-hybrid resonance, wave absorption*

## 492. Orthogonal Phase Modulation And Lissajous Mode Decoupling In Light-induced Thermoelastic Spectroscopy For Real-time Multi-component Gas Sensing

- **Score**: 0.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Rep. Prog. Phys.  |  **Feed**: Reports on Progress in Physics (ROPP)
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-18
- **Authors**: Hanxu Ma, Shunda Qiao, Ying He, Haiyue Sun And Yufei Ma
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae79a3](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae79a3)
- **Tags**: `noise`  `spectroscopy`  `experiment`  `orthogonal phase modulation`  `Lissajous mode decoupling`

### TL;DR

This paper presents a novel optical gas sensing technique using orthogonal phase modulation and Lissajous mode decoupling in light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy for real-time multi-component gas detection.

### Motivation

Real-time multi-component gas sensing is important for environmental monitoring and industrial safety, but existing methods suffer from channel crosstalk and complexity.

### Method

The authors use two continuous-wave distributed feedback lasers modulated at the same frequency with a 45° phase difference, co-exciting a quartz tuning fork. The piezoelectric signal is orthogonally demodulated via a lock-in amplifier to decouple the gas concentration information.

### Result

The sensor achieves linear responses for methane and acetylene with low systematic errors (0.54% and 0.79%) and detection limits of 0.32 ppm and 0.29 ppm, respectively.

### Conclusion

The proposed OPM-LITES sensor provides a simple and efficient solution for real-time multi-component gas detection with a novel theoretical framework for mode decoupling.

*Keywords: orthogonal phase modulation, Lissajous mode decoupling, light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy, quartz tuning fork, multi-component gas sensing*

### TL;DR

This paper studies sensory sensitivity suppression during attention shifts in the brain, a topic in cognitive neuroscience.

### Motivation

To understand how the brain adjusts sensory sensitivity during attention shifts, similar to saccadic suppression.

### Method

Experimental or computational analysis of neural or behavioral data during attention shifts.

### Result

Demonstrates that sensitivity is suppressed during attention shifts, analogous to saccadic suppression.

### Conclusion

Attention shifts involve active suppression of sensory sensitivity, which may optimize information processing.

## 539. A Hierarchical Cascade Of Sleep Rhythms Supports Motor Memory And Is Hijacked By Epileptic Spikes In Human Epilepsy

Chuahttps://ror.org/02jz4aj89department Of Advanced Computing Sciences, Maastricht University, Maastricht 6229 EN, The Netherlandsbhttps://ror.org/00za53h95department Of Neurology, Johns Hopkins University, Baltimore, MD 21205chttps://ror.org/05q6tgt32department Of Neurology, Kennedy Krieger Institute, Baltimore, MD 21205dhttps://ror.org/002pd6e78department Of Neurosurgery, Massachusetts General Hospital, Boston, MA 02114eharvard Medical School, Boston, MA 02215fhttps://ror.org/002pd6e78department Of Neurology, Massachusetts General Hospital, Boston, MA 02114ghttps://ror.org/05qwgg493department Of Mathematics And Statistics, Boston University, Boston, MA 02215hhttps://ror.org/05qwgg493center For Systems Neuroscience, Department Of Mathematics And Statistics, Boston University, Boston, MA 02215
- **Link**: [https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2517454123?af=R](https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2517454123?af=R)
- **Tags**: `memory`  `sleep rhythms`  `memory consolidation`  `epilepsy`  `neural oscillations`

### TL;DR

This paper studies sleep-dependent memory consolidation and its disruption by epileptic spikes in human epilepsy, with no connection to quantum information theory.

### Motivation

Understanding how sleep rhythms support motor memory and how epileptic activity interferes is important for neuroscience, but unrelated to quantum information or thermodynamics.

### Method

Simultaneous recordings of neural activity in cortex, thalamus, and hippocampus during sleep in epilepsy patients.

### Result

A hierarchical cascade of slow oscillations, spindles, and ripples supports memory consolidation, and epileptic spikes disrupt this process.

### Conclusion

The findings advance sleep and epilepsy research but have no relevance to quantum information theory or related fields.

*Keywords: sleep rhythms, memory consolidation, epilepsy, neural oscillations*
